The M6 options are refused until 0c. A test authorization server (`packages/server/test-support/authorization-server.ts`) makes the keys and serves the JWKS and introspection over loopback HTTP; the suites load the nine sections 9.1 names and hold one test per M3 rule, each `describe` titled `P3-E9:`, as are 0b's afd-auth suites E9 names (the capabilities, the credentials backstop, `BetterAuthAdapter` over a stubbed client). The three table rows of `spec/authorization.md` are marked done. **Deviations, approved by the coordinator 2026-11-09:** (1) the JWKS key source loads the issuer's document through a loader the host supplies, since M3 says AFD opens no socket; (2) with `auth`, AFD's own `/health` needs a token too, failing closed, and a host serves its own public health route (Loam's does); (3) a token accepted inside the clock skew after its `exp` has credentials `isVerifiedCredentials` no longer recognises, so a server with no session answers it `UNAUTHORIZED`, the safe side; (4) the `Credentials` type is exported from `@lushly-dev/afd-server`, and afd-core's `CommandContext` is unchanged; (5) the certificate binding of M3 rule 10 is unit-tested but not over a real mutual-TLS connection, a follow-up; (6) `JwtVerifier` verifies RS256 to RS512, PS256 to PS512, ES256, ES384 and EdDSA on Ed25519, Rust's list, of which 9.1's RS256, ES256 and EdDSA are the minimum; (7) with `auth`, an HTTP session (`Mcp-Session-Id` on `/message`, `/rpc`, `/batch` and `/stream`; `/mcp` keeps none) belongs to the credentials' issuer, subject and client that created it (`credentialsBinding`, M8's identity part, since M8 binds no session), and another caller's use of it is answered as an unknown session's, approved by the coordinator 2026-11-10. **After the independent security review of `f13b3fa9`** (merge after fixes): the bearer parser is one anchored match, linear in the header, where a trimming regular expression was quadratic; `JwtVerifier` refuses an introspection verifier with another audience or binding requirement, two issuers sharing one key source, and `createMcpHandler` a verifier issuer that `authorizationServers` does not name; the layer holds any verifier's credentials to its own binding requirement, this request's certificate and their `expiresAt` within the verifier's skew (none for a host verifier with no rules), so M3 rule 8's skew and deviation (3) still hold (this reading approved by the coordinator 2026-11-10); a host verifier's issuer is logged only as a visible ASCII alias; and `jwksKeySource` stops answering with keys older than `maxStaleMs` (default 1 hour) while loads fail. Section 18's place for E9, the `Build, unit and Chromium` job's `AFD's identity path` step, is not in `loam.yml` yet; until it is, the suites run in `ci.yml`. - **Step 0c** (AFD: TypeScript MCP authorization, scopes): merged in [#1050](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/1050) at `ea0cd027`, 2026-11-12; written 2026-11-10 against `main` at `dd5d3616`. 9.1's second bullet is built in `@lushly-dev/afd-server`, with a command's `scopes` in `@lushly-dev/afd-core`: static `scopes` and `requiredScopes`, a `ScopeResolver`, the 403 before dispatch for a `tools/call` on `/mcp` and `/message` (a command's own tool, grouped actions, `afd-call`, each step of `afd-batch` and `afd-pipe`) and for `/rpc`, `/batch` and `/stream`, `INSUFFICIENT_SCOPE` after routing and, with a resolver, at execution on the validated input; listing with `filterToolsByScope` (`cacheScope: "private"`); hidden commands answered as missing names on every route, `GET /stream` on a hidden mutation included (its 405 found by the independent review, 2026-11-12), and proved against a twin server without them in the individual, grouped, lazy and hybrid strategies, on `/rpc`, `/batch` and `/stream` and in the bootstrap tools; `requires` filtered; and a host's own layer. The `staticScopes` and `batches` cases load; each `describe` is titled `P3-E9:`. The table's three M6 rows are marked done; `tasks/*` stays "Planned", since TypeScript serves no tasks. **Deviations:** (1) **approved by the coordinator 2026-11-10:** a host's own layer mirrors Rust: `handler.admit(req, credentials)`, a method of the handler alone and exported from no entry, checks the credentials as a verifier's are, brands them while the handler serves the request, and refuses a request carrying a bearer token or admitted before; `handler.preDispatchCheck({ challenge, scopeSource }).decide(req, body)` is the bearer layer's own decision, and the handler takes the body read as its third argument; (2) **approved by the coordinator 2026-11-10:** `/direct`'s smallest budget, a host with the registry, the journal and `defineCommand`, rises from 39,000 to 39,100, measured 38,997 on `main` and 39,071 after (`defineCommand`'s `scopes` +31, the engine's two optional hidden-command hooks +43), against 9.1's "unchanged"; the other three stay; (3) **approved by the coordinator 2026-11-10:** first paint grows +74 and +35 and the kernel row's ceiling rises to 114,444 (13); (4) **approved by the coordinator 2026-11-10:** `/message` is decided before dispatch, since in TypeScript its answer is the body of its own response, which is the criterion M6 states (Rust has no such lane); (5) **approved by the coordinator 2026-11-10, for now:** a resource provider is not given the caller's credentials (M6, "Other methods"), since TypeScript's resource lists never vary by caller (`spec/mcp-resources.md`); the follow-up closes before any hosted server serves MCP resources whose content or existence depends on the caller, at the latest by phase 6's step 4.
- **Step 2** (`session-settle`): merged in [#1058](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/1058) at `3024a2ba`, 2026-11-12; written against `main` at `3330f86e`. Built, all in `late` but the ticket `run` gives a queued call: `kernel/src/late/session-settle.ts`, the `late-identity` chunk (13), with the listener on `auth.onAuthStateChange`; the expiry timer on the kernel's clock, re-armed when the adapter reports another `expiresAt`, a delay past 2³¹−1 ms clamped and re-armed on an early wake, `adapter.refresh()` first and a settle only when the person really changed or the session is gone; `session-settle` (internal, journaled, run top-level with boot's fields on the `palette` interface, so it is never queued and no person, agent or port reaches it) with its input as data and its result `{ person, left }`; the first settle in `provider` mode with stage A's person (the one whose records stage A read, which `kernel.ts` hands `late`), role and digest, a change from `null` in the original and the replay, and a change since stage A heard right after it; membership read again before `project-create`, a `project-update` carrying `access` and `workspace-app-set` (step 4 adds the rest of `OWNER_COMMANDS`), a changed digest settled top-level with `reason: 'membership'` before the call, then line 0 on what was read, a read or a settle that fails refusing the call `MEMBERSHIP_UNAVAILABLE`; the change of person, keyed by the settle's person and never the adapter's live one (every open overlay dismissed as an app switch, since each is the leaving person's; the active app closed by `app-close`'s rules; selections cleared; the leaving person's drafts held in memory by person and taken up again if they sign in again in the page, in `provider` mode the records reset and the next person's read, then `applyScopeChange(…, 'actor')`); every change of person (the listener's settles, the membership re-read, an `auth-*` call's change) on one chain; `SESSION_CHANGED` for a call queued across a change of person, for a copied actor (a port's, a surface's or a nested call's) that is not the person signed in, and for a call stamped as a person the shell is not settled for, with the two exemptions; `actorChanged: true` on the settle's event, on which the chrome re-reads the session, the rail, the projects section, the documents sections and the editor; a replay that never arms the timer, drives its Mock from the `session-settle` entries and replays them. Units: P3-E4's, in `kernel/src/late/session-settle.test.ts` (39); Chromium: `web/test/browser/scope/session-settle.test.ts`, the Mock's hour passed on Playwright's clock, the timer's settle journaled with its input, the restricted project left, and #864's projects list losing the name with no other call. `SESSION_50` settles nothing, so its committed journal is unchanged.
  - **Obligations closed:** the kernel's clock reaches afd-auth's `createAuthMiddleware`, in the kernel's chain and the auth gate, and the test kernels' Mocks share it (step 1's deviation 2); N4 of #919's review, a copied human actor that survived a sign-out on an excluded command, is refused `SESSION_CHANGED` (E4's change-of-person unit, "refuses a nested call whose copied person is not the session's").
  - **A refresh that fails never settles.** afd-auth's `refresh()` throws when the provider cannot be reached; the timer then asks again after 5 s, doubling to a 5-minute cap, for as long as the adapter reports the same `expiresAt` (a new one re-arms it and resets the backoff). Every enforcement point already refuses an expired session, so only the page catches up later. The adapter's own report during a refresh neither re-arms the timer nor resets its backoff; one timer is armed at a time, and nothing runs after `stop()`. A refresh that keeps an expired session settles to no one, since an expired session is no one's.
  - **Review of `b21e0022`** (independent; four blockers, five improvements, one to record), each fix with a unit that fails without it (checked by running the new units against `0a5e5b93`'s sources), in `session-settle.test.ts` but the personal store's in `scope/identity.test.ts`:
    - **B1, a change of person during stage B was never settled:** `begin()` took the adapter's person, so the person stage A hydrated for was lost. `kernel.ts` keeps the actor stage A chose the scope for and hands it to `late`; the first settle names it and the notice after it hears the change. Unit: "B1: a change of person during stage B is settled from stage A's person", and the queued-call unit now checks the Bea→Olu settle comes before the queued calls.
    - **B2, settles and `auth-*` changes were not serialized, and nothing checked the person after an await:** one chain runs the listener's settles, the re-read and `authEnd`; `change()` reads `left` once and sets the person before its first await; a settle's membership is read only for the person signed in, and the settle is dropped if a change overlapped the read (the notice that change made settles instead), while a person superseded before their settle ran settles with what the kernel holds; the re-read refuses its call `SESSION_CHANGED` when the person changed while it read; the personal store reads and writes by the settled person (`personalWrites` re-keys `get`, `list` and `key`). Units: "B2: a tab's change and a sign-out run one after another", "B2: a change of person while the membership is read refuses the call", "B2: a change to someone superseded at once settles with what the kernel holds for them"; `personalRecordId` held equal to `late`'s key.
    - **B3, a settle that failed did not fail closed:** the re-read returns the settle's result, and a failed one refuses the call `MEMBERSHIP_UNAVAILABLE`. Unit: "B3: a settle the re-read cannot make … refuses the call".
    - **B4, app-owned overlays outside the active app survived:** `change()` dismisses every owner's overlays as `app-switch`. Unit: "B4: a change of person dismisses every overlay, an inactive app's among them".
    - **I1:** `refuse()` refuses a person's or an agent's call stamped as a person the shell is not settled for (not internal, not `auth-*`, not an actor a replay restored), before line 0 and again after the re-read. The auth gate first settles a change not yet settled, so I1 refuses only a change that races the call; a session that has only expired is left to phase 2's next-call path and the timer (deviation 8). Unit: "I1: while a change of person cannot be settled, the next person's calls are refused".
    - **I2:** a change an `auth-*` call did not make (`auth-session-get`, or a call that failed) is settled with `reason: 'tab'` after its entry, from the auth gate once the call is journaled. Unit: "I2: a change of person an `auth-*` call did not make is settled after its entry".
    - **I3:** `wait()` cancels the previous timer, `arm()` returns while firing, and `retry` and the step after `refresh()` check `stop()`. Unit: "I3: an adapter that reports a change during each refresh neither double-arms the timer nor resets its backoff".
    - **I4:** the "refreshed but still expired" back-off branch was dead (`sessionActor` already reads an expired session as no one) and is removed, with this block's claim of it.
    - **I5:** before `project-create`, `project-update` and `workspace-app-set`, line 0 waits for the membership read, in `run`. Unit: "I5: line 0 decides an owner's command on the membership read before it".
    - **I6, recorded:** 4.5's "a held draft makes `beforeunload` ask" is not built (Not done, below).
  - **Re-review of `53cab644`** (confirmed the ten fixes; one blocker the fixes introduced, three improvements), each fixed with a unit that fails without it: N1's two units, the re-arm's and stage B's timeout fail against `53cab644`'s source; the others, which the re-check below found passing without their fixes, now each fail when that fix alone is taken out:
    - **N1, a same-person adapter event during the settle's read dropped the settle, and nothing settled it again:** every adapter event counted against the read, though an `expiresAt` slid on a refetch, which Better Auth's adapter emits, leaves the person as they were; the notice it made found nothing to queue, so Olu was never settled and their calls were refused. Only a change of person counts now, by the person last heard, so A→B→A still drops a read (B2). Units: "N1: an adapter event that leaves the person as they were, during the settle's read, does not drop the settle" and "N1: the same person's adapter event during the re-read does not refuse an owner's command".
    - `settleFirst`, which retries a change of person whose settle failed or was dropped, journals `reason: 'tab'`, not `'expiry'`.
    - A background notice or timer fire reports its failure, never leaving it unhandled, and a settle that fails after a refresh no longer stops the timer re-arming. Unit: "a settle that throws after a refresh is reported, and the timer re-arms".
    - **The host's membership read is bounded by `MEMBERSHIP_READ_TIMEOUT_MS`, 10 s on the kernel's clock** (the spec names no bound; the coordinator set 10 s), so stage B, which awaits the settle a change during it needs, never waits on a provider that does not answer. A read that times out has failed: before an owner's command it refuses `MEMBERSHIP_UNAVAILABLE`, and in a settle it decides with what the kernel holds. Unit: "a membership read that never answers fails after its timeout, so stage B ends".
  - **Re-check of `45931613`** (no blockers; N1 fixed, A→B→A dropped in both places, the timeout never failing open). Four fixes had units that passed without them; each now has one that fails when that fix alone is taken out, checked one fix at a time on `8358f0c3`: the A→B→A drop in a settle's read ("a change to someone else and back during the settle's read drops that read") and in the re-read ("… during the re-read refuses the owner's command"), the re-read's own timeout ("the re-read's own timeout refuses the owner's command, ignores a late answer and clears itself"), background failures reported ("a timer fire that fails after its settle is reported, never left unhandled"), and `settleFirst`'s `'tab'` ("a change whose settle failed is settled again … as another tab's"). **`stop()` now clears a waiting read's timeout**, which fired and reported for a disposed kernel ("a read's timeout goes with the kernel when it is disposed"). The units that claim a report stub `reportError`, which Node lacks, and assert it was called; stage B's timeout unit runs the one timer at exactly the read's bound. The `schedule` port's doc names the read's timeout too. Three follow-ups for step 3, when `readMembership` goes live, are in [Follow-ups](#follow-ups).

The parts step 2 adds (the ticket in `run`, the kernel's clock in `sessionActor`, `layoutInitial(id)`, `'session-settle'` in `BOOT_ONLY`, `actorChanged` in the shell and the rail) are paid for by moving code out: stage B's read of the apps with its refusals, `startInstances`' refreshes and `resetSession`'s overlay and apps resets into `late`; the review's stage A actor, in `kernel.ts`, paid for by the read's refusals; the ticket's symbol kept in `kernel.ts`, so first paint's shared chunk exports nothing new; `late-identity` imports no first-paint module (Zod, `define` and `success` reach it through the kit, as `late-editor`'s do, and it keeps its own copy of the editor's empty record, held equal by a unit), since one it imported would have parted first paint into two more chunks. The kernel alone 114,249 (from 114,444); `late` 73,475 (from 70,924, of 75,000); `late-editor` 15,304 of 15,305 (from 15,292: the editor's draft maps are made by `late` and passed in, so a change of person moves them with nothing added there); `late-identity` 7,304 of its 16,000 (13's row, now in `budget`). No ceiling raised.
  - **Deviations, each the robust choice, all approved by the coordinator 2026-11-12:** (1) `KernelPortsV1.readMembership`, optional, is the re-read's source (7.6's `whoami`, which the web host wires in step 3); absent, the kernel decides with the membership it holds. **A read that fails before a command that needs it refuses that command `MEMBERSHIP_UNAVAILABLE`, retryable and decided by the page, so a replay answers it from its entry: fail closed, never deciding an owner's command on what the kernel last held** (the coordinator's answer to the spec's silence, 2026-11-12); a read that fails during a change-of-person settle still decides with what the kernel holds, where line 0 refuses a person it does not list; (2) the web host gives no `schedule` port, since `host.ts` is first paint: `late` arms `setTimeout` on the kernel's clock when none is given, never holding a Node process open, and tests pass a fake; (3) the listener's settle has `reason: 'tab'` for every change no command made until step 3's `SessionSync` tells another tab's from the adapter's own; (4) in `provider` mode, the view-state writes made while leaving (the app closed, selections cleared) are dropped, neither the leaving person's change nor the next one's; (5) in `local` mode an `auth-*` call that changes the person moves only the drafts, since the records are everyone's, and in `provider` mode it does the whole change of person on its own call, as it settles inside itself (4.4); (6) E4's Chromium test installs Playwright's clock once the shell is open, since its boot waits on real frames. **From the review's fixes, approved by the coordinator 2026-11-12:** (7) a settle's membership is read only for the person signed in, since the host reads whoever is; a person superseded before their settle ran settles with what the kernel holds for them, so no change to a different person is merged away, and one whose read a change overlapped is dropped, the overlapping change's notice settling instead; (8) the auth gate settles a change of person not yet settled before it stamps a person's or an agent's call, as one whose settle failed, so I1's refusal is left for a change that races the call; a session that has only expired on the kernel's clock (the same person, no adapter change) is neither settled first nor refused, since it is no other person's state: phase 2's path (3.4) takes it into account at the next call and the timer settles it, so phase 2's E3 and E4 hold unchanged. *The re-review's note, accepted:* while the provider stays unreachable the timer never settles, and in that time a signed-out `layout-set` with no actor writes under the expired person's key, since keys follow the settled person; it is their screen; (9) a call that names another workspace keeps line 0 before line 1 even for a command that re-reads membership, so a non-member learns nothing from line 1.
- **Step 3** (sign-in, the probe and sessions): merged in [#1141](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/1141) at `4e11c2ce` (2026-11-13); built 2026-11-13 on `main` at `f7ecbd43`. Built:
  - **The probe (4.2):** `web/src/identity.ts`, first paint: once `deployment.json` names a `provider` identity whose `path` matches the projection's pattern, the page reads `<base_path><path>/loam/whoami` with a 1,500 ms bound, parses the answer strictly (anything malformed is signed out), and boots the kernel with `DeferredAuthAdapter`, which reports the probe's state and hands its listeners to the real adapter when stage B loads it. A probe answer that arrives after the bound is applied as an adapter event. The web host's `readMembership` and `watchMembership` ports are the probe's.
  - **The `auth` chunk (4.1, 4.3):** stage B, `provider` only. Better Auth's client with its passkey and organization plugins, `BetterAuthAdapter` started from the probe's state, Loam's session capability over `loamServerPlugin`'s endpoints (handles, never tokens), the passkey capability keeping the provider's codes (a dismissed ceremony is `PASSKEY_CANCELLED`) and calling `signalUnknownCredential` after a removal, and `SessionSync` on the `loam-session` channel, so a change of person in one tab reaches every tab of the profile. The page reads the session again when it becomes visible, comes online, and every 60 s while visible, and tells the kernel membership may have changed.
  - **At the provider (4.3, 4.6, 5.8):** `identity/src/sessions.ts`. Each session records how it was made (`loamMethod`: passkey, password or oidc). `GET /loam/sessions` lists the person's sessions by handle (the first 16 hex digits of SHA-256 over the token), with `current`, a device label from the user agent and no address, and needs a session, not a fresh one. `POST /loam/revoke-person` takes a handle, `others` or `all`; `others` and `all` need a fresh session (created within `freshAge`, 300 s) made with a passkey when the person holds one, else `REAUTH_REQUIRED`; it deletes the sessions, clears this cookie when it signed this session out, and answers `{ revoked, signedOut }`. The same freshness rule guards the passkey plugin's register-options, verify-registration and delete endpoints, by a before-hook, and is checked again in `registration.afterVerification` on the session read again from the store; Better Auth's own session and credential routes are not served (5.8). A register-options request for a join or recovery context with a session present is `SIGN_OUT_FIRST`. `whoami` gains `warning`: `IDENTITY_ORGANIZATION_UNKNOWN` to anyone, `IDENTITY_BINDING_MISMATCH` to owners only (2.3, 15).
  - **The kernel (2.1, 4.6, 5.2):** `kernel/src/late/identity-commands.ts`, in `late-identity`: `session-list`, `session-revoke`, `account-passkey-list`, `-add`, `-rename` and `-remove`, with the codes of 15 (`REAUTH_REQUIRED`, `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `PASSKEY_*`, `SIGN_IN_METHOD_UNAVAILABLE`, and anything else the provider answers as `IDENTITY_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`, retryable). They are recorded commands: a replay answers each from its entry, and one with no record stops as `REPLAY_NOT_RECORDED`. `HUMAN_COMMANDS` (3.4) answer `HUMAN_REQUIRED` on the agent interface in both modes, before any other refusal. `auth-sign-in` answers `SIGN_IN_METHOD_UNAVAILABLE` for a method the deployment does not offer. Signed out in `provider` mode, `app-list` answers `[]` and `palette-search` only the signed-out commands, and each sign-out clears the `~signed-out` records (step 1's step 3 obligations). In `local` mode the Mock's session and passkey capabilities are attached (`withMockCapabilities`), which closes step 1's deviation 12.
  - **The chrome (4.2, 5.1, 14):** the signed-out main ("Sign in to <organization>" and its button), the not-a-member and binding notices; the title bar's account button, a menu button in `provider` mode; `loam-sign-in-dialog` (the `sign-in` chunk), a native modal dialog with "Sign in with a passkey" first, the email field with `autocomplete="username webauthn"` and the conditional ceremony where the browser has it, the password form and one button per OIDC provider, by the deployment's `methods`; a failed or cancelled ceremony is announced in its live region and focus stays on the button pressed; `loam-account-menu` (the `account-menu` chunk), the person's name and email and "Sign out", in the title bar's banner landmark. A sign-out returns the address to the workspace root.
  - **Step 2's follow-ups, all three closed, and `beforeunload`:** a settle's membership read checks `now()` on wake and waits again when the timer ran early; a new `session.id` under the same person counts as a change, so an A→B→A merged inside one poll is not missed, and a slid expiry keeps the id; after a settle's read fails, the person's membership is read again on the refresh backoff (5 s doubling to 5 minutes), and a changed answer settles with `reason: 'membership'`. A held draft with unsaved tabs makes the page's `beforeunload` ask (4.5, the review of step 2's I6). After a provider sign-in is journaled the kernel reads the person's membership, and the next call waits for it.
  - **The harness:** `serveDeployment({ authHost })` serves the shell over HTTPS on `localhost` with a certificate made per run, proxies `api/auth/` to the test-only auth host, and `launchForDevHost({ spki })` trusts it; `addVirtualAuthenticator` drives Chromium's DevTools `WebAuthn` domain.
  - **Tests:** P3-E1's, E3's, E4's (the post-sign-in read), E10's, E11's and E15's units and Chromium scenarios: `kernel/src/late/identity-commands.test.ts`, `identity/src/sessions.test.ts`, `web/test/browser/identity/sign-in.test.ts` and `revocation.test.ts`. `call-scope.test.ts` gains the six commands' inputs.
  - **Tests amended, each to what step 3 changes:** `scope/identity.test.ts` and `late/session-settle.test.ts` give the `provider` fixture `methods: ['passkey', 'password']`, since a credentials sign-in to a passkey-only deployment is now `SIGN_IN_METHOD_UNAVAILABLE`; step 2's N1 units slide the expiry under the same session id (a new id is now a change); three step 2 units answer the post-sign-in membership read their scenario now makes (B2's superseded settle filters `reason: 'tab'`, the d-bea flip answers Olu's own read first, and the timeout unit hangs on the second read).
  - **First paint 294,515 bytes and 91,544 gzip, +5,693 and +2,234 against `main` at `f7ecbd43` (288,822 and 89,310), past step 3's allowance of 1,950 and 800 (13).** Raised under D115, each to its measurement plus a margin, in `budget.mjs`: `jsGzip` 91,000 to 92,000; `lateIdentityMinified` 16,000 to 24,000 (22,629, from 7,304: the six commands, `HUMAN_REQUIRED`, the follow-ups and the Mock's capabilities). New rows: `authMinified` 68,767, set at 72,000 (13 estimated 58,500); `signInMinified` 8,489, set at 9,300 (estimated 5,000); `accountMenuMinified` 3,543, set at 3,900. `late` 73,968 (+493) and the kernel alone 114,402 (+153) are within their rows. The probe and `DeferredAuthAdapter` are about 2,300 of the first-paint growth; afd-auth's errors, listeners and session state, which `auth` shares, are pinned into first paint's `events` group with FAST's `ref`, since a lazy chunk importing them split new first-paint chunks.
  - **Deviations, each the robust choice:** (1) the passkey plugin's `registration.requireSession` stays `true` until step 4, whose join and recovery pages are the only registrations without a session; step 3's hooks enforce 5.8 on top of it, so no build serves registration without its checks (step 1's deviation 1 said step 3); (2) `revoke-person` deletes sessions only: consents and refresh tokens join it in step 7, with the OAuth provider (step 1's deviation 7); (3) the kernel cannot answer `REAUTH_REQUIRED` before asking, since it does not hold a session's creation time: it maps the provider's answer; (4) `PASSKEY_LAST` treats recovery as `none` until step 4 builds recovery: a person's last passkey is refused when every method is a passkey; (5) the probe starts once `deployment.json` is read, not beside it, since the path is the deployment's; E10 holds first paint to that one added request; (6) the sign-in dialog is a native modal `<dialog>`, not routed through the overlay manager, which holds the kernel's overlays: the browser's top layer gives the focus trap and Escape; (7) an autofill sign-in (`autoFill: true`) does not mark a sign-in running, since the conditional ceremony waits indefinitely and would hold every change of person; (8) the listener's settle keeps `reason: 'tab'` (step 2's deviation 3 stands): `SessionSync` delivers a refresh, not the reason, so another tab's change and the adapter's own read look alike to the kernel; (9) E3's two tabs of one profile cannot both run a shell, since the journal's writer lock holds one per profile: a second device is a second browser context, and the profile's other tab is a page listening on the `loam-session` channel; (10) the conditional (autofill) ceremony itself is not completed in Chromium, whose virtual authenticator does not answer it: a Chromium scenario holds that the dialog starts it only where the browser offers it, and a unit that a waiting autofill holds no change of person; (11) the account section's passkey list is step 4's, so E1 adds the passkey by `account-passkey-add`; the account menu has no "Account" item until step 4; (12) `whoami`'s answer gains `warning`; (13) the first-paint `events` group gains afd-auth's `errors`, `listeners`, `session-state` and `types` and FAST's `templating/ref.js` (above); (14) the sign-in dialog marks its buttons `aria-disabled` while a ceremony runs, never `disabled`, since a disabled button drops focus.
  - **Trust-boundary review of `db426168`** (approvable once fixed; two blockers, three improvements, four nits), each fixed with a unit that fails without it, but N1:
    - **B1, revoking by handle needed no fresh session,** so a stale session revoked every other session one handle at a time where `'others'` was refused. `revoke-person` now needs freshness for any session but the caller's own. **4.6 amended by the coordinator on 2026-11-13 from this review:** "freshness for revoking any session other than the caller's own, by handle, `'others'` or `'all'`".
    - **B2, Better Auth's own `/list-sessions` and `/revoke-session` were served:** the first answered every session's token, the second revoked without D99's rule, even from a stale session. Both are disabled, with every other Better Auth route that touches sessions or credentials and that Loam does not need; 5.8 lists each route served or not, with its reason. The reviewer's probe against the real provider, rerun: each bypass now answers `401 REAUTH_REQUIRED` or `404`, and every victim session stays signed in.
    - **Found while rerunning that probe:** a cookie's request from another origin reached `revoke-person` (`200`), because Better Auth 1.7.18 skips its origin and CSRF checks when `NODE_ENV` is `test`, as the test host's child and the units run. Production would have checked; Loam now sets both checks on explicitly, and a unit holds `403 INVALID_ORIGIN`.
    - **I1:** the web host's membership read refuses a `whoami` that answers for a person other than the one the session holds (another tab's sign-in before this tab's adapter has heard); the kernel reads again on its backoff.
    - **I2:** the human commands are left out of `mcpOptions().commands`, and `HUMAN_REQUIRED` refuses them on every interface but the palette's, where the chrome and the person's own calls run; no app port may call them (`APP_CALLABLE_SHELL_COMMANDS_V1`).
    - **I3:** registration's after-verification check reads the session again with `getAuthoritativeSessionFromCtx`, and refuses when none comes back.
    - **N1:** only a passkey call is an autofill. Its unit passes without the fix as well, since `auth-sign-in`'s input schema strips `autoFill` from any other method before the scope sees it; the method check is the second guard. **N2:** the real adapter starts from the state when it is built. **N3:** the `beforeunload` unit unstubs in a `finally`. **N4:** the afd-auth modules pinned into first paint's `events` group (`errors`, `session-state`, `listeners` with `types`) measure 2,494 bytes minified, 1,288 gzip as separate chunks, unpinned in a throwaway build; they were first paint on `main` already, through the Mock (afd-auth's rendered bytes in first paint 8,985 on `main`, 9,208 here), so the pin keeps three chunks out of first paint rather than adding the code.
    - **Deviation 2's guard:** a unit fails if an OAuth-provider, OIDC-provider, consent, MCP or JWT plugin is registered while `REVOKES_CONSENTS` is `false`, so step 7 cannot serve consents before `revoke-person` revokes them.
  - **Re-check of `6ac9324b`** (revise; every earlier finding and the CSRF change closed), each fixed with a unit that fails without it, checked one fix at a time:
    - **NEW-1, the freshness rule refused every passkey holder, even straight after a passkey sign-in:** `loamMethod` is `returned: false`, so the session Better Auth hands a route (`parseSessionOutput`) never carries it, and `requireFreshPasskeySession` never saw `passkey`. The rule now reads the session's own row by its token (`adapter.findOne`, which keeps the field; `internalAdapter.findSession` parses it away) and decides `createdAt` and `loamMethod` from it, for `revoke-person`, the passkey routes' before-hook and registration's after-verification check alike. Units at the provider: a holder's fresh passkey session revokes a handle and the others, starts a registration and deletes a passkey; the same holder's stale passkey or fresh password session is refused each. `registration.test.ts` no longer mocks a session that carries `loamMethod`. E3 in Chromium: a person adds a passkey, signs in by the virtual authenticator's passkey, revokes a password device by handle and a third by `others`, and that password device is refused revoking the passkey one.
    - **NEW-2, single sign-on matched a route that is not served:** genericOAuth's providers sign in on the core `/callback/:id`, not `/oauth2/callback/`, so OIDC sessions were marked by none and a first OIDC sign-in could not create its user (5.6); both failed closed. `methodOfPath` and the user-create hook now match `/callback/`, with the provider from the `id` parameter or the resolved path.
    - **NEW-3:** `/get-session` answers without `session.token` (an after-hook); nothing in afd-auth's adapter, `web/src` or the chrome reads it.
    - **NEW-4:** a disabled path with a parameter is blocked by its pattern, so `/reset-password/<token>` answers 404.
    - **NEW-5:** 5.8 names `/ok` and `/error`, and every served organization route with the rule that holds it. **`/organization/leave` is no longer served, a coordinator amendment from this re-check (2026-11-13):** Better Auth's leave skipped the organization hooks, so a member who left kept every session and Loam's last-owner check never ran (probed: `200`, both sessions still signed in); it now answers 404, and Loam's own leave endpoint comes with `member-leave` (phase 6's A4). The other organization routes are recorded as Better Auth's own rules, with no code change, and step 4 re-checks them (Follow-ups).
- **Step 4** (the account section, members and owners): merged in [#1184](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/1184) at `3c0a3b25` (2026-11-15); built 2026-11-13 on `main` at `4e11c2ce`. Built:
  - **At the provider (5.6, 5.7, 5.8, 7.5):** passkey registration runs with `requireSession: false`, and `loamServerPlugin` puts back what that drops (`identity/src/registration.ts`): `resolveUser` names the join's reserved user or the recovery token's person and writes nothing; `afterVerification` re-reads the session, applies every row of 5.8, refuses a join or recovery without `createSession: true`, and does the join's writes (the user with its reserved id and a verified email, the member row, the invitation accepted, the token consumed) or the recovery transaction, through the plugin's transaction (`getCurrentAdapter`), so they commit with the passkey and the session or not at all. A session made by a join's or a recovery's registration is marked `passkey`; one made by `join/password` or `recover/password`, `password`. Invitations (`sendInvitationEmail`) mail a single-use join link, 32 random bytes stored as their SHA-256 in `loam_join`, expiring with the invitation (48 hours), with the user id it reserves; sending one again replaces the link. `GET /loam/join/check` shows the invitation to the link's holder (`ACCOUNT_EXISTS`, then `SIGN_OUT_FIRST`), and `POST /loam/join/password` joins with a password where `methods` allows one. Recovery (`identity/src/recovery.ts`): `POST /loam/recover/request` answers `{ sent: true }` 1,000 ms after the request whatever it found, mails only a verified account, at most three an hour (then drops silently), a new link cancelling the outstanding one, each 15 minutes; `GET /loam/recover/check`; `POST /loam/recover/password`; and the single sign-on ceremony, `POST /loam/recover/oidc/start` (signs out this browser, sets the `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Lax` nonce cookie for 300 s, its hash in `loam_recovery_nonce`) and `POST /loam/recover/oidc/complete` (the nonce unconsumed and within 300 s, a session made after it within 300 s by that provider's sign-in, the provider's report of a verified email equal to the account's, read from the ID token Better Auth keeps on the account; it consumes the nonce, runs the recovery transaction keeping this session and marks it `recovery` for 300 s in `loam_recovery_mark`). The recovery transaction deletes every other session and sets `recoveredAt` on the provider's user, and the announcement goes to the account's verified address once the transaction has committed. 5.8's first row accepts a `recovery`-marked session for a registration, never for a removal or a revocation. `bootstrapOrganization` (`identity/src/bootstrap.ts`) creates the organization, pins the binding and mails the first owner's join link; once a binding exists it sends the first owner a new link while the organization has no member, and refuses once it has one (5.6); the test-only auth host seeds through it. `GET /loam/account` (the person's organizations, bound one marked, membership, ways in, `recovery`, `recoveredAt`), `GET /loam/members` (emails to an owner only) and `GET /loam/invitations` (the person's own, with each organization's slug and name).
  - **The kernel (3.4, 5.1, 5.3, 5.4, 7):** `late/member-commands.ts`, in `late-identity`: `account-get`, `account-update`, `account-invitation-list`, `-accept` and `-reject`, `member-list`, `member-invite`, `member-invitation-list` and `-cancel`, `member-remove` and `member-role-set`, every one recorded (3.4). `OWNER_COMMANDS` is the frozen list of 3.4's fourth row; each is refused `HUMAN_REQUIRED` off the palette with the human commands, left out of `mcpOptions().commands`, preceded by the membership re-read (4.4, 7.6) and decided by `isOwner` after line 0 (`OWNER_REQUIRED`); only an `owner`-role member grants, removes or invites the `owner` role. After `account-invitation-accept`, `member-remove` and `member-role-set` the kernel reads the person's membership again and the next call waits for it. Every call to the provider names the bound organization by its id. The provider's codes are kept or mapped (15): `MEMBER_LAST_OWNER` from Loam's hook and from Better Auth's own two errors, Better Auth's "only owner" refusal `OWNER_REQUIRED` for an admin removing an owner. #852's rule (7.3, 7.4): `project-create` of a listed slug and `project-update { access }` on a listed project are an owner's (`OWNER_REQUIRED`), and an agent's (or any agent's with `access`) `HUMAN_REQUIRED`; Q2 as answered (D110): the creator or an owner changes a project's members, anyone else may only remove themselves; an owner may leave themselves out of a restricted project and add themselves to one they cannot see; `PROJECT_MEMBER_NOT_PERSON`; `PROJECT_SLUG_TAKEN` is `PROJECT_SLUG_UNAVAILABLE`, one code and message for a slug held, visible or restricted, and `all`; `workspace-app-set` is an owner command. `PASSKEY_LAST` reads `AccountV1.recovery`. `section-open` takes `account`.
  - **The chrome and the web host (5.1 to 5.7, 14):** the `account` chunk (`chrome/src/identity/account.ts`): `loam-account-section` at `/<workspace>/~/account[/<tab>]`, a tablist of links over `profile`, `passkeys`, `sessions` and `members` (`passkeys` and `sessions` in `provider` mode; an owner's controls absent for anyone else), captioned tables, buttons naming their rows, destructive actions confirmed in a modal dialog with the consequence in words, `REAUTH_REQUIRED` met by a passkey sign-in and one retry; `loam-account-invitations` under main's not-a-member notice; and the join and recovery pages, top level, over the provider alone. The account menu gains "Account"; the sign-in dialog gains "Can't sign in?" and, per single sign-on, "Can't sign in? Use <provider>". The web host's `auth` chunk gives the adapter Loam's account capability and an organization capability that keeps the provider's codes, and serves the link pages' provider; `main.ts` renders `~/join` and `~/recover` from the `account` chunk with no kernel.

    - **B1, a member removed others by repeating an id:** D110's "remove only yourself" compared lengths, so Cy's `[Bea, Bea]` dropped Dee. A member list naming anyone twice is now `VALIDATION_ERROR` for every caller, with a suggestion, and the rule compares sets: the members held, less the caller, are exactly those asked. Units: Cy's `[Bea, Bea]`, and a repeated id for an owner and the creator.
    - **I1, a joined person's email shown to members as their name:** 5.6 amended above (decided by the coordinator). Unit: after a join, no field a member who is no owner sees of the others holds any part of another person's email.
    - **I2, nothing tested that the `recovery` mark never deletes a passkey or revokes others:** a provider test in which a passkey holder's `recovery`-marked session calls `/passkey/delete-passkey` and `/loam/revoke-person { target: 'others' }`, each `REAUTH_REQUIRED`. With `allowRecovery` widened to every path it fails, and with it widened for `revoke-person` alone it fails on the revocation.
    - **I3, nothing tested the provider binding in `oidc/complete`:** a second test OpenID provider; a ceremony begun for `corp` and signed in through `other` is `REAUTH_REQUIRED`. Without the `loamProvider` check it fails.
    - **I4, single use rested on the database serializing transactions:** the recovery token (at verification and in `recover/password`), the join token and the nonce are consumed by a conditional update that must change exactly one row (`consumeOnce`), and `recover/request` creates the new link, then cancels every other outstanding one by a conditional update; on a database that runs transactions side by side, racing requests may still each leave one outstanding, which the final check's IMPROVEMENT 3 closes by cancelling the rest when one is used. Units: three concurrent `recover/password` calls with one token answer one 200 and two 404, racing requests leave at most one link (SQLite serializes, so these hold either way), and a second consumption of a row is refused (this one fails without the condition).
    - **I5, a first owner's lapsed link left the deployment stuck:** 5.6 and 10.2 amended above. Units: a second call while the organization has no member ends the first link and sends a working one; once it has a member, and for another slug, it refuses.
    - **N1:** a provider's answer the kernel does not map is `IDENTITY_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`, retryable only for a 5xx or no answer; a 4xx is not retryable (the web host's capabilities carry the status). **N2:** 5.8 says `/cancel-invitation` lets an admin cancel an owner invitation, and that it only denies. **N3:** the join and recovery pages remove the token from the address bar once read (Chromium, E17). **N4:** 5.7 says the nonce is marked consumed, as the code does.

  - **The record and the commands (6.1, 6.3):** `kernel/src/late/persona.ts`, in `late-identity`: `persona-list` (a query an agent may run for its person; `default` first, synthesized with the title "Default"), and `persona-create`, `-update`, `-remove` and `-set`, human commands (`HUMAN_REQUIRED` to an agent, as `HUMAN_COMMANDS` already listed). Rows in the `persona` table, read and written only for `personOf(context)`: another person's persona, or an unknown id, is `PERSONA_NOT_FOUND`, with the same message. `PERSONA_LIMIT` at the ninth; `PERSONA_INVALID` for an emphasized app that is not an instance, an avatar not in `ICONS_V1`, a landing naming both a section and an app or neither, a repeated name, a changed or removed `default`, and a malformed or taken id. Each code has a suggestion and is in `LoamKernelErrorCode`. Undo as 6.3 says: `persona-create`'s is `persona-remove`; `persona-update`'s restores the four fields; `persona-remove`'s is `persona-create` with the same id and fields, so `persona-create` takes an optional `id`; `persona-set`'s sets `previous`.
  - **The active persona (6.1):** `loam.persona~<person>` holds `{ active }`. Stage A reads its person's (`kernel.ts`) into a map of each person's active persona; `late-identity` keeps the map as personas are set and removed and as people change. `persona-set` applies a persona's density and theme by calling `appearance-set`'s handler in the same call, then writes the record; it notes `personaChanged`, and moves no scope and no main. Removing the active persona makes `default` active in the same call.
  - **The context field (6.2):** `persona`, `dev.loam/persona`, journaled, in `KERNEL_CONTEXT_FIELDS` and not in `MCP_CONTEXT_FIELDS`. `execute` sets it from the map for the call's person (the copied or agent actor's, else the session's), only when that person's active persona is not `default`; no decision reads it, and an app's handler context is built field by field, so no app is given it.
  - **At sign-in (6.2):** `session-settle`'s change of person reads the arriving person's persona, enters its `project` through `decideAccess` (`scope.allows`) and its `landing` through `canOpen`, after the scope; either refused leaves what the person had, silently.
  - **Emphasis (6.2):** `railOrder`, `bandFor`'s comparator, puts the active persona's `emphasis.apps` first in their band in the order named; `loam-app-entry`'s `emphasized` state draws a mark beside the icon (`ButtonText` in forced colors, `HighlightText` on the current entry) and is described "Emphasized by your persona". `palette-search` puts the emphasized apps' commands, and the commands of the emphasized sections, first, after the search's own limit, so it answers the same items with or without a persona.
  - **The chrome (6.3, 14):** the shell reads `persona-list` when stage B ends, at each change of person and each persona change; a `persona-set` announces "Persona: <title>" in the live region and places the rail again. The account menu gains the persona switch: a group labelled "Persona" of `menuitemradio`, `default` first, the active one `aria-checked`, with the selected state's start-edge bar.
  - **What other members see (6.1):** `presentationOf(person)`, the active persona's `presentation` alone (`{}` for `default`), for `member-list`.

The command sweeps' inputs moved to `kernel/src/scope/call-inputs.ts`, shared by `call-scope.test.ts` and the persona sweep, with the five commands' inputs.

  - **Deviations, each the robust choice:** (1) `loam.persona~<person>` is a row of the view-state table, but never registered with the view-state registry, so `view-state-get`, `-set` and `-list` answer it as missing: through `view-state-set` it would bypass `persona-set`'s checks, and listed it would tell a persona apart where 6.2's sweep compares `view-state-list`; (2) a persona's landing and project apply at a `provider` sign-in, the change of person `session-settle` makes; a `local` deployment's Mock sign-in moves nothing, since its records are everyone's (4.5); (3) the rail paints its first frame in its own order, and stage B's re-read places the emphasized apps first, unannounced, as `persona-list` is a late command; (4) `defaults.project` and `defaults.landing.app` are not checked when a persona is made, only at sign-in, since checking a slug would tell its maker whether a restricted project exists; (5) a `persona-set` journals its own entry with the persona before it, and the calls after it with the new one; (6) the account menu's first item, which takes focus when it opens, is now the switch's `default`, so `sign-in.test.ts`'s E11 expects a `menuitemradio` there; (7) a dimmed, emphasized entry is described by both, why it is dimmed first (`aria-describedby="why emphasis"`, the review's N3); (8) the palette orders emphasized items after the search's own limit, so it answers the same items with or without a persona, and an emphasized command past the limit does not move into view (the review's N4); (9) the umbel server's `components-brief` was not available to this session (its MCP server failed to connect), so the markup follows `loam-design` alone; (10) the sweep's two personas share their density, project and landing: it signs the person in again after `auth-sign-out`, and at a `provider` sign-in a persona's project and landing move them by design, so different ones would compare two places rather than two personas.

    - **B1, an agent's call carried the wrong persona:** `execute` looked the persona up from the agent interface's actor, which gains its `onBehalfOf` only in the chain, so an agent's calls carried the signed-out person's persona in `local` mode and none in `provider` mode, and the sweep's agent half compared none. `execute` now keys the lookup on a copied actor's person, else the session's. Units: a signed-in person's agent journals that person's persona; the sweep asserts every path's own entry carries its twin's persona. **Proved in a throwaway copy:** with `project-list` leaking the call's `persona` into its result, all four halves fail (both modes, person and agent); with B1's fix reverted and no leak, both agent halves fail on the journal assertion.

Chromium: with the next person's `persona-list` answering 400 ms late, as `provider` mode's does behind the membership read, no frame after the change shows the leaving person's title or emphasis (checked to fail without the clear).
    - **I3:** in forced colors the mark is the entry's own system color in each state: `ButtonText` resting, `HighlightText` hovered, pressed or current, `GrayText` dimmed, each named with `forced-color-adjust: none`. **Deviation from the ruling's `currentColor`:** under `forced-color-adjust: none`, Chromium resolves `currentColor` to the author color (measured: the mark `rgb(65, 70, 81)` against the entry's forced `rgb(0, 0, 0)`), so the colors are named per state, as the entry's own forced-colors rules name them. Chromium: resting, hovered and dimmed, the mark is the system color its state names, each resolved in the page.
    - **I4:** `persona-set` with the active persona changes nothing: no appearance, no `personaChanged`, no undo. Unit.
    - **I5:** the sweep runs in `provider` mode too, the person a member and no owner, in a project restricted to them, so line 0 and the owner checks answer.
    - **N1:** `persona-create` with another person's id answers as a malformed id does; only the caller's own id has its own message. Unit. **N2:** a switch's undo restores the appearance it replaced (6.3, amended). Unit. **N3, N4:** deviations 7 and 8.
- **Step 6** (agent members): merged in [#1269](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/1269) at `39b03ea3` (2026-11-16 UTC), after the trust-boundary review, final check and coordinator verification of its fixes. All seven checks passed on `44c38583` ([Loam run](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/actions/runs/43789038464)); the coordinator reran the agent-member unit file on Node 26, 34 passed. Built:
  - **The record and the commands (8.1, 8.2):** `kernel/src/late/agent-members.ts`, in `late-identity`: `agent-member-add`, `-update` and `-remove` (owner commands, already in `OWNER_COMMANDS`: the membership re-read before each, `OWNER_REQUIRED`, `HUMAN_REQUIRED` off the palette, not served over MCP), `agent-member-confirm` and `-decline` (human commands, the sponsor alone, `AGENT_MEMBER_SPONSOR_ONLY`), and `agent-member-list` and `-get` (queries an agent may run for its person, served over MCP, recorded as 3.4's first row says). Ids are `agt_` and 20 of `[a-z0-9]` from the call's random. A record is a row of the `agent-member` table and never a user, session or credential at the provider. Undo: `-add`'s is `-remove`; `-remove`'s and `-update`'s restore the record they replaced, kept in the store's `agent-member-undo` table under an opaque `agu_` id that the undo names alone (`-add { undo }`, `-update { agent, undo }`), used once (deviation 15).
  - **The sponsor rules (8.3, D111):** before `-add` and `-update` (when it names `sponsor` or `access`), top-level from the scope middleware's `run`, after the owner's membership re-read and line 0, the kernel reads the sponsor at the provider (`/loam/members`) and journals it as `membership-settle { person, role, digest }` (4.4), which sets that person's role in the kernel's `MembershipV1` and never the session's person; a read or settle that fails refuses the call `MEMBERSHIP_UNAVAILABLE`. The handler then decides from what was journaled, so a replay reads no provider: the sponsor a person member (`AGENT_SPONSOR_INVALID`, the same for no member, a former one and an `agt_` id), who may enter every project named (`AGENT_SPONSOR_INVALID`, naming the projects to the owner calling, a missing project answered as one they may not enter). Projects are named by id or slug and kept as ids. The status: `active` when the caller is the sponsor, else `pending`; on `-update`, a new sponsor or wider access (`widens`: the workspace, `sponsor`, or a project not held) asks again; a suspended record is reactivated only by naming a sponsor.
  - **Line 4 (3.3):** `decideAccess`'s step 4, from step 1, now reads the table this step keeps (`scope.agents`, written through by every write). **Deviation 1** puts it on every command an `agt_` actor makes but the kernel's own, in `late/scope-change.ts`'s `run`, before the membership re-read, so a pending, suspended or unknown agent member is allowed nothing and never reaches the provider; an app's command, and a shell command acting at the call's scope (`AT_CALL_SCOPE`), is decided at the call's scope, and every other shell command at the workspace's, so one with `workspace: false` runs no workspace-level command, whatever project it names (3.3 placed it only where phase 2's line 2 runs, app commands and the shell commands at the call's scope, which left `project-create` or `member-list` open to a pending one). An actor acting for no member is left to line 0, whose refusal says so. It is decided on the actor afd-auth's middleware stamped, never `agentActor()`'s.
  - **Suspension (8.3, 4.4):** a read that finds a sponsor no member of the workspace notes them, and a top-level `agent-member-settle { agents }` after that call writes `suspended: 'sponsor-left'`, the next person's or agent's call waiting for it (`SessionSettle.follow`): after `member-remove` (whose `suspended` now lists them), after the sponsor's read before `-add` or `-update`, after a members read (`member-list`, `agent-member-list`, `-get`) that no longer holds a sponsor, and after a `session-settle` that finds the person signed in no member. `provider` mode alone, and never in a replay, which replays the entries. `membership-settle` and `agent-member-settle` are in `BOOT_ONLY` (so no person, agent or port reaches them) and were already in `KERNEL_SETTLE_COMMANDS`.

  - **At the provider:** `/loam/members` answers each person's membership digest beside their role, the one their own `whoami` answers (a hash of what the row shows), which `membership-settle` journals; `member-list` leaves it out of its rows, as it does the member id, so they are `MemberV1`. **No route is enabled**: an agent member has no account, so 5.8 is unchanged; a unit lists every `/loam/*` route from the provider instance and finds none for an agent.

- **Step 7** (identity over MCP, connecting an external agent): merged in [#1908](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/1908) at `09f3b0c0` (2026-11-22 UTC); built 2026-11-21 on `main` at `0ab76415`. Built:
  - **The server kernel's chain (9.5):** `createKernelParts` takes `gate: 'principal'` (`KernelPartsOptions`, not `createKernel`'s), which refuses anything but `provider` mode (`IDENTITY_MODE_INVALID`) and opens the palette, agent and boot registries with `principalGate` in place of afd-auth's middleware, so a port call, a nested call and a handler's client go through the same gate as the top-level call. The gate and the registry that mints principals are `kernel/src/server/principal-gate.ts`, a lazy chunk a browser never loads, importing nothing first paint shares. `KernelParts.admitPrincipal` mints a principal (frozen; its membership one read-only row, the person's; refused when the actor, person and connected agent disagree or a person's id starts `agt_`); the gate trusts only one its own kernel minted, sets `context.actor` from it whatever the caller sent, and refuses any other call `UNAUTHORIZED`, but boot's own work (`BOOT_FIELDS`) and `startInstances`' refresh (`INSTANCE_FIELDS`, `app-instance-refresh` alone), which `execute` marks under the symbol `KERNEL_OWN` after the caller's fields, so no field can claim to be one. `KernelCallFields.principal` carries it, never journaled, never on an app handler's context. Kernel-wide membership on such a kernel is `NO_MEMBERS`, so a read that skips the call's principal refuses rather than decides; `ScopeRuntime.membershipOf(call)` and `agentOf(call)` read the principal (in a browser, the kernel's `MembershipV1` and `agent-member` table, as before), and `allows`, line 0, line 4, `member-list`'s emails and the agent-member rows read them. No session logic with the gate: no `session-settle`, `membership-settle` or `agent-member-settle` registered, no hearing of the adapter, no change-of-actor step (no `CALL_MARK`), no page settle, no page-event middleware in `mcpOptions()`, and `chooseScope` sets the workspace without the adapter.
  - **Identity over MCP (9.2, 9.3), in `loam-identity`:** `createMcpAuth` (AFD's `JwtVerifier` on `EdDSA` with the provider's own keys through `providerKeys`, the audience the one MCP resource, at most 600 s; 9.2's steps 1 and 2 around it, `PRINCIPAL_ISSUER_UNKNOWN` and `AGENT_CANNOT_SIGN_IN`, each a 401; `requiredScopes: ['loam:read']`, `loam:write` for every mutation and for a command the registry does not hold, `filterToolsByScope: false`); `principalOf` (9.2's steps 3 to 6: the connected agent by client and subject, `active`, else `AGENT_NOT_CONNECTED`; the subject's member row read for this request with no cache); `credentialsGate`, the handler's first middleware; `createServerKernel` over `createNoSessionAdapter()` with the account capability read by each call's person (`serverAccountAccess`: `readAccount`, `readMembers` and `readInvitations`, the functions the session endpoints now share, and the connected agents; its writes refuse, each a human command).
  - **Connected agents (9.2, 5.3, D98):** `loamServerPlugin`'s `loam_connected_agent` table (`AgentMemberV1`'s shape, `kind: 'connected'`, access `{ workspace: true, projects: 'sponsor' }`, the scopes approved; no token or secret); `connectAgent`, run by a `before` hook on `/oauth2/consent` when it accepts, refusing a non-member (`WORKSPACE_NOT_MEMBER`) and a client an owner removed for this person (`AGENT_DISCONNECTED_BY_OWNER`); `/loam/connected-agents` (any member), `/disconnect` (the sponsor alone, deleting the record, the client's consent and its refresh tokens) and `/remove` (an owner: kept `suspended: 'owner'`, or deleted with `forget`). `revoke-person`'s `all` deletes every consent of the person's and revokes their refresh tokens (`REVOKES_CONSENTS` is now true), and the recovery transaction suspends their connected agents and ends their connections. The kernel gains `account-agent-list` and `account-agent-disconnect` (human, recorded, not served), connected agents beside installed ones in `member-list`, `agent-member-list` and `-get`, and `agent-member-remove` of a connected agent (with `forget`) at the provider, recorded by what it names (`recordedCall`: an id the kernel's table does not hold).
  - **The authorization server (9.4):** `oauth.ts`: the JWT plugin (`EdDSA`), `oauthProvider` (the one resource, seeded `overwrite` and linked to every client; 600 s access and 30-day refresh tokens; `authorization_code` and `refresh_token`; PKCE for every client; dynamic registration off; the login and consent pages under the shell) and `@better-auth/cimd` with MCP 2026-07-28's profile over `@better-auth/cimd/node`'s resolve-once transport, which a host may replace. A hook refuses `require_pkce: false` and `skip_consent` on every client route; `clientPrivileges` lets only an owner of the bound organization manage a client; Better Auth's page routes that register or change a client, and `/oauth2/update-consent`, are disabled. `@better-auth/oauth-provider` and `@better-auth/cimd` 1.7.18 are pinned beside `better-auth`.
  - **The pages (9.4, 5.3):** `chrome/src/identity/oauth-pages.ts` in the `account` chunk, routed by `web/src/main.ts` at `~/oauth/login` and `~/oauth/consent` with `createOAuthProvider` in the `auth` chunk; the account section's agents tab. The umbel MCP server did not connect in this session, so `components-brief` was not called; the pages use native controls and the account chunk's styles, no `violet-*` component, as step 6's tab did.
  - **Tests:** P3-E8 in `identity/src/mcp-identity.test.ts` (20, through a test-only handler: a real `createMcpHandler` on loopback over `createKernelParts({ gate: 'principal', auth: <a Mock signed in as an owner listed in the restricted project> })`, real tokens from the provider in this process); `kernel/src/server/principal-gate.test.ts` (13); P3-E8's kernel half in `late/agent-members.test.ts` (3); P3-E20's provider half and step 4's follow-up in `identity/src/oauth.test.ts` (9); P3-E20 in Chromium, `web/test/browser/identity/oauth.test.ts` (5, through the test-only auth host, which now pre-registers clients and serves the four account pages' response policy). `call-scope.test.ts` takes the two new commands' inputs.
  - **Figures,** measured in one tree against `main` at `0ab76415`: first paint 326,696 to 328,238 (+1,542) and 102,796 to 103,170 gzip (+374, within its row); the kernel alone 137,347 to 141,140; `late-identity` 62,702 to 64,976; `auth` 71,703 to 72,707; `account` 25,727 to 30,973. Under D115 `jsMinified` 326,800 to 328,800, `kernelMinified` 137,400 to 141,700, `lateIdentityMinified` 63,000 to 65,500, `authMinified` 72,000 to 73,200, `accountMinified` 28,000 to 31,500.
  - **The step 5 review's B1, step 7's half (closed):** on the server no value is derived from an actor before the gate stamps it: `execute` derives a nested call's principal from its copied actor alone, and only a principal this kernel minted for that very actor object (never a `principal` field), and the gate then stamps the actor from the principal, so nothing keys on a caller's actor. Units: a forged principal, one another registry minted, a copied actor, a `{ ...actor }` copy, and a human actor beside an agent's principal, each refused or stamped as the principal's.
  - **Deviations, each the robust choice:** (1) `execute` reads a nested call's principal from its copied actor, not a copied `principal` field at each port (9.5 named `checkedCall`, the nested client, the surface port and the refusals), so no path can drop or mismatch it; (2) the login page resumes at the provider's own `/oauth2/authorize` with the request less its signature and its `prompt` and `max_age`, which the sign-in just satisfied, and the provider checks it again with the session; instead of `oauthProviderClient`'s fetch plugin, so no Better Auth client plugin joins the `auth` chunk and the page follows no target of its own; (3) a pre-registered client's host is empty, and the consent page and agents tab say "registered by the deployer"; (4) a direct disconnect of another person's connected agent is `AGENT_MEMBER_SPONSOR_ONLY` (403), not `OWNER_REQUIRED`, since an owner removes one with `/remove`; (5) `skip_consent` is refused on every client route as `require_pkce: false` is (9.4 named only PKCE), since the consent page is where a connection is made; (6) `clientPrivileges` and the disabled page routes for client registration, which 9.4's list of registration paths implies; (7) E8 runs the provider in the test's own process (`createTestProvider`), not the child host, and the MCP client is afd-client's `StreamableHttpTransport` with a bearer header; (8) `others` ends a person's grants as `all` does, and both suspend their connected agents, as 4.3 says (9.2 had named only `all`'s consents); (9) a member's removal at the provider (`afterRemoveMember`) also ends their grants and suspends their connected agents, so a refresh token never outlives a membership; (10) A4 and A8 (`PROVIDER_HOOK_FIELDS`, `agent-member-settle` on the server) and A6's `persona-list` are phase 6's step 5, which builds the hook and the transport; the gate's marks leave room for the third.
  - **Trust-boundary review of `f5695d9e4`** (two independent reviewers from one brief: Claude Opus 5.5 at `high`, 2 blockers and 10 improvements; GPT-6.2 Astra at `high`, 11 blockers and 2 improvements; both "approvable once fixed"). Each fixed with a unit that fails without it, unless named otherwise:
    - **An owner's removal undone by its sponsor** (both): `/disconnect` and `account-agent-disconnect` refuse a record `suspended: 'owner'` (`AGENT_DISCONNECTED_BY_OWNER`), the tab offers no Disconnect on it, and the connections end before the record goes, so a failure never leaves a consent with no record. Units in E8 and the kernel's.
    - **The consent hook wrote before the provider validated** (both), **and two racing approvals could make two rows** (both): the `before` hook only refuses (a non-member, an owner's removal), and an `after` hook writes the record when the provider answered a redirect with a code, with the scopes the stored consent holds; `principalOf` admits nothing for two rows of one person and client, and `connectAgent` keeps the oldest. Units: a consent asking a scope the client never requested connects nothing; two rows answer `AGENT_NOT_CONNECTED`.
    - **Grants that survived revocation** (Astra): deviations 8 and 9. Units for `others` and for a removal.
    - **Account reads that fell back to the adapter's person** (Astra) and **the server still reading the adapter at boot and in `app-list`** (both): a gated kernel reads an account only through `accountFor`, by the call's person, and refuses `IDENTITY_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` without it, never `local`'s answer; stage A's persona and appearance reads, `app-list`'s signed-out empty rail, the queue ticket's person and the personal store are off with the gate. E8 and the kernel's units now count the adapter's reads from before the kernel is built: none.
    - **A nested client outliving its request** (Astra): `credentialsGate` retires the principal when its request ends (`KernelParts.retirePrincipal`), so neither it nor its actor admits a later call. Unit.
    - **The provider and the kernel naming different workspaces** (Astra): `createServerKernel` refuses a binding's workspace that is not the kernel's (`IDENTITY_BINDING_MISMATCH`); E8's provider binds the kernel's own workspace. Unit.
    - **A signing key trusted after it was retired** (Astra): `providerKeys` is AFD's `jwksKeySource`, which bounds how long loaded keys are used. No unit of Loam's: AFD's suite covers the source.
    - **The login page** (Astra): it offers each `oidc:<id>` method, returning to itself and resuming; a client's `prompt=login` with a session present asks for a sign-in and then resumes without looping; it follows only `http(s)` addresses (Opus). Chromium case for `prompt=login`; the single sign-on button has no Chromium case (Follow-ups).
    - **Paging** (Astra): the connected agents are read page by page, and `readMembers` reads the caller's row directly and its page from the database in a stable order with a count, where it searched the first hundred rows. No unit past a hundred rows.
    - **The host's route inventory** (Astra; `pnpm verify` found it too): the authorization server's routes are listed by intended access, the admin routes and OpenID discovery checked unreachable over HTTP, and `/oauth2/delete-consent` and `/oauth2/client/rotate-keys` disabled.
    - **The instance refresh's mark lost when queued** (Opus): `INSTANCE_FIELDS` never joins the queue, as `BOOT_FIELDS` never does. **Owner checks in `project-*`** read the call's membership (Opus). **Provider failures** in `agent-member-remove` and `-get` of a connected agent answer `IDENTITY_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` with a suggestion (Opus). **Another person's scopes** are no longer in `/loam/connected-agents` (Opus). **Discovery tools** are an explicit set in the resolver (Astra); unit. **`mcp()`** marks its commands `expose.mcp` (Opus).
    - **Not changed, with reasons:** E8's listed-slug `project-create` and a real `startInstances` refresh on a gated kernel (Opus, and Astra's improvement) need a team instance and a mounted instance on the server, which phase 6's steps 4 and 6 bring; the handler's rule is the agent interface's, unit-tested by step 4, and P3-H5(b) proves it over MCP (Follow-ups). The settings', limits' and autonomy's `isOwner` and the agent-member owner commands still read the kernel's membership (Opus): none is served over MCP, they fail closed on a gated kernel (`NO_MEMBERS`), and phase 6's step 5 brings the first human principal on the server (Follow-ups). Opus's question on confirmations: no confirmation command is served over MCP (`mcpOptions()` leaves out `confirmation-*` and every `setting` command), so no MCP call raises one.

Each tab names its app, "Greetings (Hello)", by the owner line's rule (`ownerTitle`, moved into the kernel's own `owner-title` module and entry: never a name that says the shell's, a twin's with its instance), and the panel says it again ("From Hello, an app, for this workspace."). The surface shows at `{ workspace }`, whatever project is active: a native element through the app surfaces' `host()` (`ensureActivated`, then `createMount` with no document, as a region named by the section and its app), a view's `main` resource in a `loam-view-frame` from the view chunk whose broker is `port.surface({ scope: { workspace } })`'s. It stays while its tab shows, so a re-read recreates nothing, but a surface that could not show is tried again; another tab or the section's leaving drops it, and a view is hidden, told and removed only once it has answered (phase 1's 6.2), the section with it. A change of person, a sign-in or out, a session or membership settle, or a change of main's gate drops it and reads every tab again; the app's own settings reach it, as main's do (phase 4's 3.8); each `show()` and `read()` is a generation, so a slower read never replaces a newer one. A reload straight into the tab waits for the app surfaces, at most 15 s, as main does. `account-surface.ts` reads the same table as `accountSurfaceOf`, with no DOM, for its unit. `SurfaceHostV1.createMount`'s `document` is optional. As for every native surface, the boundary is the surface port's, not the module's: a native app's `activate` context keeps its own port (D54).

Its Chromium scenarios: `web/test/browser/identity/app-account.test.ts`, in a `local` deployment with Atlas active: hello's tab, "Greetings (Hello)", renders `hello-main` under axe; its client's `scope-get` answers the workspace and no project; `hello-list` naming Atlas's scope, or `level: 'project'`, is `SCOPE_NOT_GRANTED` with a suggestion; `hello-greet` runs as `caller: 'hello'`, journaled at `{ workspace }`; the workspace's greeting changed reaches the surface in place; its modal says why it was refused; another tab drops the mount and the tab brings it back; a reload straight into the tab shows hello. A sign-in replaces the surface made before it, and main's gate closing takes every app's tab away. As a view, the frame's `scope-get` answers the workspace; a `hello-greet` whose `_meta` names Atlas answers and is journaled at `{ workspace }`; `dev.loam/level: 'project'` is `SCOPE_NOT_GRANTED`; another tab takes the frame away. (2) 5.5's "a declared surface of a native or view app" is read as one that shows a single thing: a native app's `main`, `inspector` or custom `sidebar` element, or a view app's `main` resource. A panel holds several views, a list or tree sidebar and the rail, header, status and keybindings are the shell's, so none is a section. (3) `MANIFEST_ACCOUNT_LEVEL`, a refusal 5.5 does not name, for an app whose `scope_levels` lack `workspace`: 5.5 says the section runs at workspace level, which such an app does not, as `ui.project_summary` is refused in an app without `project`. (4) `MANIFEST_ACCOUNT_LIMIT` is the schema's `maxItems` mapped in `refusal-map.ts`, not a rule, since the schema refuses a third section before any rule runs. (5) For a view, "its port refusing any other scope" holds as phase 2's broker does: a scope a view names in `_meta` is not refused but never used, since the broker is closed over the surface's scope; for a native app the call is refused. (2) **E8's instance refresh:** the hosted server's gated kernel never calls `startInstances`, since it serves no app's command (`HOSTED_MCP_COMMANDS`; phase 6's spec lists `app-instance-refresh` as not served there) and the relay discovers an instance's catalog itself; its `mountTransport` port refuses outright (`host/src/mcp.ts`, `serverRefusal('mounted instance')`), so the server cannot refresh an instance, and the case has nothing to run there. It is proved on a real principal-gated kernel with a mounted instance (`kernel/src/apps/mounted.test.ts`: the refresh runs with no principal, journaled with no actor, and the same command called without one is `UNAUTHORIZED` on both interfaces), and on the profile the gated kernel journals no instance refresh and nothing with no actor but boot's `workspace-deployment-apply`, while what reaches the instance with the deployer's bearer is the relay's discovery and the calls it forwards; section 18's E8 gains "Hosted" for both cases. (3) **P3-H4's expiry is the verifier's:** `createMcpAuth` keeps AFD's 60 s clock skew past `exp` (`DEFAULT_CLOCK_SKEW_S`), so "accepted until it expires and refused after" is checked as accepted right after the revocation, before `exp`, by a sample each revocation's own test takes, never past `exp` plus the skew, and first refused after `exp`, by `invalid_token`. Each revoked request is sent with what the person held before the revocation (the session's cookie, and the refresh token of the second of two rotations, the first rotated token's grant shown to work, which is then `invalid_grant`), never the browser's jar, which a sign-out clears. (4) **P3-H4's MCP refusal at once is `AGENT_NOT_CONNECTED` in both cases:** `session-revoke 'all'` suspends the person's connected agents (4.3), and so does a removal (step 7's deviation 9); the token layer still answers the token (200) until it expires, which is the row's last clause. (5) **P3-H2 runs E1's whole passkey path on the profile** (its failed and cancelled ceremonies keeping focus, the autofill's start as R8 amended it, no ceremony's bytes in either journal, and the reload's probe answering the person), **and E17's passkey path:** the enrolment by the mailed link with no password and no session, the link refused a second time and after its expiry (its row aged), a join registration with another person's session present refused `SIGN_OUT_FIRST` at option generation and its page saying "Sign out first", a link for an email that already has an account refused `ACCOUNT_EXISTS` at generation and its page saying "You already have an account" with no passkey, membership or accepted invitation made, a direct sign-up refused, and `REAUTH_REQUIRED` for a session older than 300 s and for a passkey holder's password session. E17's clauses outside the passkey path (an OIDC sign-in with `email_verified: false`, accepting an invitation unverified) stay local, since the profile has no OIDC provider; and three of its direct-request clauses stay local, proved by `identity/src/enrolment.test.ts` and `registration.test.ts` against the same `createLoamAuthOptions` the profile mounts unchanged: a token not consumed by option generation alone, a `join:` verification without `createSession: true`, and the fault injected after `afterVerification`, which needs a test hook the profile does not have.
- **Not done:** nothing of the plan: phase 3 exited 2026-11-26. What stays open is in [Follow-ups](#follow-ups), each row filed as an issue at the exit, #2771 to #2786, sub-issues of #1541.
- **Follow-ups:** [Follow-ups](#follow-ups).

## What this phase serves

- **Principles:** 3, everything is a command; 5, scope is shell-level; 7, identity once; 8, agents are first-class users; 13, deterministic core; 15, AFD first; 19, live sessions revalidate when membership changes (its seam, not the service).
- **Decisions it depends on:** D4 (the active project narrows, never grants); D11 (identity once on `@lushly-dev/afd-auth`, Better Auth first, persona never a grant, the persona id in Loam's context fields); D15 (AFD first); D32 (single-tenant, workspaces bound to the provider's organizations); D36 (the hosted server on Hono, Better Auth among its routes); D40 (agent members: a human sponsor, installed by an owner, scoped to projects, not a seat, unable to sign in or administer; identities here, delegation in phase 5); D46 (membership epochs); D54 (native apps are trusted code); D55 and its consequence (phase 3 must restrict `project-create` for a slug a team instance lists, and such a project's members, to an owner); D56 (a `stdio` instance on the hosted server by the deployer's configuration, **narrowed by D95**, the owner's decision of 2026-11-09, to refuse one in a `provider`-mode workspace); D63 and D73 (`workspace-app-set`, which "phase 3 restricts to an owner"); D64 (a test-only stand-in, named as such wherever its result is recorded); D71 and D72 (scope, the order of a call, `applyScopeChange`); D77 and D83 (the ports). The architecture glossary's **Sponsor**: "the person accountable for an agent member and named in its `onBehalfOf`".
- **Decisions this specification records:** D84 to D94, decided with this specification's approval on 2026-11-09 (D87 and D90 subject to Q2 and Q4); D95 to D98, decided by the owner on 2026-11-09; D99, decided by the coordinator on 2026-11-09; D110 and D111, decided by the owner on 2026-11-12 (Q2 and Q4, each as recommended); all in [Amendments](#amendments-to-the-architecture-and-the-decision-log).

## Terms
